# Brussels

This article is about the Brussels-Capital Region. For the municipality within it, which is the de jure national capital, see [City of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Brussels). For other places, see [Brussels (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_\(disambiguation\)).

"Bruxelles" redirects here. For the Canadian community, see [Bruxelles, Manitoba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruxelles,_Manitoba).

**Brussels**,[^cite_note-fn1-13] officially the **Brussels-Capital Region**,[^cite_note-fn2-14][^cite_note-Belgian_Constitution-15][^cite_note-CIRB-creation-16] is a [region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communities,_regions_and_language_areas_of_Belgium#Regions) of [Belgium](3343.md) comprising [19 municipalities](#Municipalities), including the [City of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Brussels), which is the capital of Belgium.[^cite_note-article_194-17] The Brussels-Capital Region is located in the central portion of the country. It is a part of both the [French Community of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Community_of_Belgium)[^cite_note-décret-18] and the [Flemish Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Community),[^cite_note-19] and is separate from the [Flemish Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Region) (Flanders), within which it forms an [enclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enclave), and the [Walloon Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walloon_Region) (Wallonia), located less than 4 km (2.5 mi) south.[^cite_note-decreet-20][^cite_note-21]

Brussels grew from a small rural settlement on the river [Senne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senne_\(river\)) to become an important city-region in Europe. Since the end of the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War), it has been a major centre for [international politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_politics) and home to numerous international organisations, politicians, [diplomats](28165911.md) and [civil servants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_servant).[^cite_note-BBC_Belgium-22] Brussels is the *[de facto](43594.md)* capital of the [European Union](9317.md), as it hosts a number of principal [EU institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EU_institutions), including its [administrative-legislative](9974.md), [executive-political](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council), and [legislative](9581.md) branches (though the judicial branch is located in [Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxembourg_City), and the European Parliament meets for a minority of the year in [Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strasbourg)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemey2007-1][^cite_note-12010E/PRO/06-23][^cite_note-24] Because of this, its name is sometimes used [metonymically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metonymy) to describe the EU and its institutions.[^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] The secretariat of the [Benelux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benelux) and the [headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_Headquarters) of [NATO](21133.md) are also located in Brussels.[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-28]

Brussels is the most densely populated region in Belgium, and although it has the highest [GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP_per_capita),[^cite_note-\:4-29] it has the lowest disposable [household income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Household_income).[^cite_note-statbel-30] The Brussels Region covers 162 km<sup>2</sup> (63 sq mi) and has a population of over 1.2 million.[^cite_note-\:8-31] Its five times larger [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_metropolitan_area) comprises over 2.5 million people, which makes it the [largest in Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_areas_in_Belgium).[^cite_note-BelMun2008-32][^cite_note-BelMetr-33][^cite_note-\:5-34] It is also part of a large [diamond-shaped conurbation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Diamond), extending towards the cities of [Ghent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghent), [Antwerp](32149462.md) and [Leuven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leuven), as well as the province of [Walloon Brabant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walloon_Brabant), in total home to over 5 million people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_MeeterenBoussauwDerudderWitlox2016-35] As Belgium's economic capital and a top financial centre in Western Europe with [Euronext Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euronext_Brussels), Brussels is classified as an *Alpha-* [global city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city).[^cite_note-36] It is also a national and international hub for rail, road and air traffic,[^cite_note-37] and is sometimes considered, together with Belgium, as Europe's geographic, economic and cultural crossroads.[^cite_note-38][^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] The [Brussels Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Metro) is the only [rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) system in Belgium. In addition, both [its airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Airport) and [railway stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_railway_stations_in_Belgium) are the busiest in the country.[^cite_note-\:2-41][^cite_note-42]

Historically Dutch-speaking, Brussels saw a [language shift to French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francization_of_Brussels) from the late 19th century.[^cite_note-43] Since its creation in 1989, the Brussels-Capital Region has been officially bilingual in French and Dutch,[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45] although French is the majority language and *[lingua franca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingua_franca)*.[^cite_note-BruS13EN-46] Brussels is also increasingly becoming multilingual. English is spoken widely and many migrants and [expatriates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expatriate) speak other languages as well.[^cite_note-BruS13EN-46][^cite_note-\:7-47] The Region is governed by the [Dilliès government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilli%C3%A8s_government), a broad bilingual coalition of seven parties.[^cite_note-\:10-48]

Brussels is known for its cuisine and gastronomic offer (including its [local waffle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waffle#Brussels), its [chocolate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_chocolate), its [French fries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_fries) and its numerous types of [beers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_in_Belgium)),[^cite_note-49] as well as its historical and architectural landmarks; some of them are registered as [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](44940.md).[^cite_note-50] Principal attractions include its historic [Grand-Place/Grote Markt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand-Place) (main square), *[Manneken Pis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manneken_Pis)*, the [Atomium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomium), and cultural institutions such as [La Monnaie/De Munt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Monnaie/De_Munt) and the [Museums of Art and History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Museums_of_Art_and_History). Due to its long tradition of [Belgian comics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_comics), Brussels is also hailed as a capital of the [comic strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comic_strip).[^cite_note-Comic-3][^cite_note-\:1-51]

## Toponymy

See also: [Names of European cities in different languages (B)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_European_cities_in_different_languages_\(B\))

### Etymology

The most common theory of the origin of the name *Brussels* is that it derives from the [Old Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Dutch) *Bruocsella*, *Broekzele* or *Broeksel*, meaning 'marsh' (*bruoc* / *broek*) and 'home, settlement' (*sella* / *zele* / *sel*) or 'settlement in the marsh'.[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-vgc-53] Saint [Vindicianus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vindicianus), the Bishop of [Cambrai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambrai), made the first recorded reference to the place *Brosella* in 695,[^cite_note-54] when it was still a [hamlet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamlet_\(place\)). The names of all the municipalities in the Brussels-Capital Region are also of Dutch origin, except for [Evere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evere), which is possibly [Celtic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celtic_languages) or [Old Frankish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Frankish).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETribotDefawe20063-55]

### Pronunciation

In French, *Bruxelles* is pronounced [\[bʁysɛl\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/French) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/6/67/Fr-Bruxelles.ogg/Fr-Bruxelles.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/6/67/Fr-Bruxelles.ogg/Fr-Bruxelles.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup> (the *x* is pronounced [/s/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) and the final *s* is silent) and in Dutch, *Brussel* is pronounced [\[ˈbrʏsəl\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Dutch) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/d/d0/Nl-Brussel.ogg/Nl-Brussel.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/d/d0/Nl-Brussel.ogg/Nl-Brussel.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>. Inhabitants of Brussels are known in French as *Bruxellois* (pronounced [\[bʁysɛlwa\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/French) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/c/c1/Fr-bruxellois.ogg/Fr-bruxellois.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/c/c1/Fr-bruxellois.ogg/Fr-bruxellois.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>) and in Dutch as *Brusselaars* (pronounced [\[ˈbrʏsəlaːrs\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Dutch)). In the [Brabantian dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brabantian_dialect) of Brussels (known as [Brusselian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brusselian_dialect), and also sometimes referred to as [Marollian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marollian_dialect)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETreffers-Daller199425-56] they are called *Brusseleers* or *Brusseleirs*.[^cite_note-evans-57]

Originally, the written *x* was pronounced [/ks/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English). In the [Belgian French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_French) pronunciation as well as in Dutch, the *k* eventually disappeared, as reflected in the current [Dutch spelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_spelling), whereas in the more conservative [French form](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_orthography), the spelling remained.[^cite_note-58] The pronunciation [/ks/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) in French only dates from the 18th century, but this modification did not affect the traditional Brussels usage. In France, the pronunciations [\[bʁyksɛl\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/French) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/8/8b/Fr-Bruxelles.oga/Fr-Bruxelles.oga.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/8/8b/Fr-Bruxelles.oga/Fr-Bruxelles.oga.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup> and [\[bʁyksɛlwa\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/French) (for *bruxellois*) are often heard, but are rather rare in Belgium.[^cite_note-Lerond,_1980_pp._477-59]

## History

Main article: [History of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Brussels)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Brussels).

### Early history



[Charles of Lorraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles,_Duke_of_Lower_Lorraine), traditionally considered the founder of what would become Brussels, c. 979

The history of Brussels is closely linked to that of [Western Europe](33800.md). Traces of human settlement go back to the [Stone Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Age), with vestiges and place-names related to the civilisation of [megaliths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalith), [dolmens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolmen) and [standing stones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_stones) (Plattesteen near the [Grand-Place/Grote Markt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand-Place) and [Tomberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomberg_metro_station) in [Woluwe-Saint-Lambert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woluwe-Saint-Lambert), for example). During [late antiquity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_antiquity), the region was home to [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) occupation, as attested by archaeological evidence discovered on the current site of [Tour & Taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_&_Taxis), north-west of the [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentagon_\(Brussels\)) (Brussels' city centre).[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61] Following the decline of the [Western Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Roman_Empire), it was incorporated into the [Frankish Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankish_Empire).

According to local legend, the origin of the settlement that was to become Brussels lies in Saint [Gaugericus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaugericus)' construction of a chapel on [an island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-G%C3%A9ry_Island) in the river [Senne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senne_\(river\)) around 580.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEState2004269-62] The official founding of Brussels is usually said to be around 979, when [Charles, Duke of Lower Lorraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles,_Duke_of_Lower_Lorraine), transferred the [relics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relic) of the [martyr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyr) Saint [Gudula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gudula) from [Moorsel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moorsel) (located in today's province of [East Flanders](152405.md)) to Saint Gaugericus' chapel. When [Otto II, Holy Roman Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otto_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor), appointed the same Charles to become Duke of [Lower Lotharingia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Lotharingia) in 977,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERiché1983276-63] Charles ordered the construction of the city's first permanent fortification, doing so on that same island.

### Middle Ages

[Lambert I, Count of Louvain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambert_I,_Count_of_Louvain), gained the County of Brussels around 1000 by marrying Charles' daughter. Because of its location on the banks of the Senne, on an [important trade route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brabant_Road) between the [Flemish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_of_Flanders) cities of [Bruges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruges) and [Ghent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghent), and [Cologne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cologne) in the [Kingdom of Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Germany), Brussels became a commercial centre specialised in the textile trade. The town grew quite rapidly and extended towards the upper town (Treurenberg, [Coudenberg/Koudenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coudenberg), and [Sablon/Zavel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sablon,_Brussels) areas), where there was a reduced risk of floods. As the town grew to a population of around 30,000, the surrounding marshes were drained to allow for further expansion. In 1183, the Counts of Leuven became [Dukes of Brabant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dukes_of_Brabant). Brabant, unlike the county of Flanders, was not fief of the king of France but was incorporated into the [Holy Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire).

In the early 13th century, the [first walls of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_walls_of_Brussels) were built[^cite_note-64] and after this, the city grew significantly. Around this time, work began on what is now the [Cathedral of St. Michael and St. Gudula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_St._Michael_and_St._Gudula) (1225), replacing an older [Romanesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanesque_architecture) church.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrigode1938185–215-65] To let the city expand, [a second set of walls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_walls_of_Brussels) was erected between 1356 and 1383. Traces of these walls can still be seen; the [Small Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_Ring,_Brussels), a series of boulevards bounding the historical city centre, follows their former course.

### Early modern



View of Brussels, c. 1610

In the 14th century, the marriage between heiress [Margaret III, Countess of Flanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margaret_III,_Countess_of_Flanders), and [Philip the Bold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_the_Bold), [Duke of Burgundy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_of_Burgundy), produced a new Duke of Brabant of the [House of Valois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Valois), namely [Anthony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony,_Duke_of_Brabant), their son.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlockmansPrevenier199930–31-66] In 1477, the Burgundian duke [Charles the Bold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_the_Bold) perished in the [Battle of Nancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Nancy).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKirk1868542-67] Through the marriage of his daughter [Mary of Burgundy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_of_Burgundy) (who was born in Brussels) to Holy Roman Emperor [Maximilian I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilian_I,_Holy_Roman_Emperor), the [Low Countries](51402.md) fell under [Habsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habsburg) sovereignty.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEArmstrong1957228-68] Brabant was integrated into this composite state, and Brussels flourished as the Princely Capital of the prosperous [Burgundian Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgundian_Netherlands), also known as the [Seventeen Provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seventeen_Provinces). After the death of Mary in 1482, her son [Philip the Handsome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_the_Handsome) succeeded as Duke of Burgundy and Brabant.

Philip died in 1506, and he was succeeded by his son [Charles V](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_V,_Holy_Roman_Emperor) who then also became [King of Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_Spain) (crowned in the [Cathedral of St. Michael and St. Gudula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_St._Michael_and_St._Gudula)) and even Holy Roman Emperor at the death of his grandfather Maximilian I in 1519. Charles was now the ruler of a [Habsburg Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habsburg_Empire) "on which the sun never sets" with Brussels serving as one of his main capitals.[^cite_note-Capital-2][^cite_note-69] It was in the [Coudenberg Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coudenberg_Palace) that Charles V was declared of age in 1515, and it was there in 1555 that he abdicated all of his possessions and passed the [Habsburg Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habsburg_Netherlands) to King [Philip II of Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_II_of_Spain).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWasseige19954-70] This palace, famous all over Europe, had greatly expanded since it had first become the seat of the Dukes of Brabant, but it was destroyed by fire in 1731.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMardaga1994222-71][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWasseige19956–7-72]



The [Grand-Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand-Place) after [the 1695 bombardment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardment_of_Brussels_\(1695\)) by the French army

In the 16th and 17th centuries, Brussels was a centre for the [lace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lace) industry. In addition, [Brussels tapestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_tapestry) hung on the walls of castles throughout Europe.[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74] In 1695, during the [Nine Years' War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nine_Years'_War), King [Louis XIV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_XIV) of France sent troops to [bombard Brussels with artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardment_of_Brussels_\(1695\)). Together with the resulting fire, it was the most destructive event in the entire history of Brussels. The Grand-Place was destroyed, along with 4,000 buildings—a third of all the buildings in the city. The reconstruction of [the city centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentagon_\(Brussels\)), effected during subsequent years, profoundly changed its appearance and left numerous traces still visible today.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECulotHennautDemanetMierop1992-75]

During the [War of the Spanish Succession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_the_Spanish_Succession) Brussels fell in Anglo-Dutch hands following the [Battle of Ramillies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ramillies) in 1706. In 1708, Brussels again sustained a [French attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assault_on_Brussels_\(1708\)), which it repelled. Following the [Treaty of Utrecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Utrecht) in 1713, Spanish sovereignty over the Southern Netherlands was transferred to the Austrian branch of the House of Habsburg. This event started the era of the [Austrian Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_Netherlands). Brussels [was captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Brussels) by France in 1746, during the [War of the Austrian Succession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_the_Austrian_Succession),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETucker2009753-76] but was handed back to Austria three years later. It remained with Austria until 1795, when the Southern Netherlands were captured and annexed by France, and the city became the [chef-lieu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefectures_in_France) of the [department of the Dyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyle_\(department\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOudiette1804XIII-77][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuvergier1835300-78] The French rule ended in 1815, with the defeat of [Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon) on the [battlefield of Waterloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Waterloo), located south of today's Brussels-Capital Region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalloyHayt200686–90-79] With the [Congress of Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_Vienna), the Southern Netherlands joined the [United Kingdom of the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands), under King [William I of Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_I_of_the_Netherlands). The former Dyle department became the province of [South Brabant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Brabant#United_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands), with Brussels as its capital.

### Late modern



*Episode of the [Belgian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Revolution) of 1830*, [Gustaf Wappers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustaf_Wappers), 1834

In 1830, the [Belgian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Revolution) began in Brussels, after a performance of [Auber's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel-Francois-Esprit_Auber) opera *[La Muette de Portici](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Muette_de_Portici)* at the [Royal Theatre of La Monnaie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Monnaie).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESlatin197953–54-80] The city became the capital and seat of government of the new nation. South Brabant was renamed simply [Brabant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Brabant), with Brussels as its administrative centre. On 21 July 1831, [Leopold I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopold_I_of_Belgium), the first [King of the Belgians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_the_Belgians), ascended the throne,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPirenne194830-81] undertaking the destruction of the city walls and the construction of many buildings.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemey200996-82]

Following independence, Brussels underwent many more changes. It became a financial centre, thanks to the dozens of companies launched by the *[Société Générale de Belgique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9rale_de_Belgique)*. The [Industrial Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Revolution) and the opening of the [Brussels–Charleroi Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels%E2%80%93Charleroi_Canal) in 1832 brought prosperity to the city through commerce and manufacturing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECharruadas2005-83][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemey200996–97-84] The [Free University of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_University_of_Brussels_\(1834%E2%80%931969\)) was established in 1834 and [Saint-Louis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Louis_University,_Brussels) in 1858. In 1835, the [first passenger railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_rail_transport_in_Belgium) built outside England linked the municipality of [Molenbeek-Saint-Jean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molenbeek-Saint-Jean) with [Mechelen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mechelen).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWolmar201018–20-85]



The [Place Royale/Koningsplein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_Royale,_Brussels) in the late 19th century

During the 19th century, the population of Brussels grew considerably; from about 80,000 to more than 625,000 people for the city and its surroundings. The Senne had become a serious [health hazard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_hazard), and from 1867 to 1871, under the tenure of the [city's then-mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_the_City_of_Brussels), [Jules Anspach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Anspach), its entire course through the urban area was [completely covered over](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Covering_of_the_Senne).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemey199065-86] This allowed [urban renewal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_renewal) and the construction of modern buildings of *[Haussmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haussmann's_renovation_of_Paris)-esque* style along grand [central boulevards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Boulevards_of_Brussels), characteristic of downtown Brussels today.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEggericx19975-87] Buildings such as the [Bourse Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bourse_Palace) (1873), the [Palace of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Justice,_Brussels) (1883) and [Saint Mary's Royal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Mary's_Royal_Church) (1885) date from this period. This development continued throughout the reign of King [Leopold II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopold_II_of_Belgium). The [International Exposition of 1897](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_International_Exposition_\(1897\)) contributed to the promotion of the infrastructure.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchroeder-GudehusRasmussen1992-88] Among other things, the [Palace of the Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa_Palace), today's [Royal Museum for Central Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Museum_for_Central_Africa), in the suburb of [Tervuren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tervuren), was connected to the capital by the construction of the [Avenue de Tervueren/Tervurenlaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avenue_de_Tervueren), an 11 km-long (6.8 mi) grand alley.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETemmerman19952–4-89]

Brussels was one of the major European centres of the [Art Nouveau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Nouveau_in_Brussels) style in the 1890s and early 1900s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECulotPirlot2005-90] The architects [Victor Horta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Horta), [Paul Hankar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Hankar), and [Henry van de Velde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_van_de_Velde), among others, were known for their designs, many of which survive today.[^cite_note-\:6-91]

### 20th century



The 1927 [Solvay Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solvay_Conference) in Brussels was the fifth world physics conference.

During the 20th century, the city hosted various fairs and conferences, including the [Solvay Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solvay_Conference) on Physics and on Chemistry, and three [world's fairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_fair): the [Brussels International Exposition of 1910](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_International_Exposition_\(1910\)), the [Brussels International Exposition of 1935](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_International_Exposition_of_1935) and the 1958 Brussels World's Fair ([Expo 58](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expo_58)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchroeder-GudehusRasmussen1992-88] During [World War I](4764461.md), Brussels was [an occupied city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_occupation_of_Belgium_during_World_War_I). It escaped large-scale destruction,[^cite_note-92] although the [interwar period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interwar_period) would still see a significant amount of (re)construction.[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94] In November 1918, after the [German Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_revolution_of_1918%E2%80%931919) had broken out, Brussels was embroiled in street battles between revolutionary soldiers who wanted to end the occupation and their imperialist counterparts; additionally, explosives left behind by retreating German troops damaged infrastructure and around 2,300 houses.[^cite_note-95]

During [World War II](32927.md), Brussels was [occupied by German forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_occupation_of_Belgium_during_World_War_II) once again. While it was again spared major damage, a deadly bombing took place on 7 September 1943. It was carried out by the [American airforce](32090.md), which had tried to aim at [SABCA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SABCA) facilities in [Haren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haren,_Belgium), but accidentally struck an area in [Ixelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ixelles), killing 282 civilians.[^cite_note-96] The city was liberated by the British [Guards Armoured Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guards_Armoured_Division) on 3 September 1944. [Brussels Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Airport), in the suburb of [Zaventem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaventem), traces its roots to [a German military airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melsbroek_Air_Base) that was built in nearby [Melsbroek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steenokkerzeel).[^cite_note-97]



British tanks [arrive in Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_Belgium) on 4 September 1944, ending the [German occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_occupation_of_Belgium_during_World_War_II)

After World War II, Brussels underwent extensive modernisation. The construction of the [North–South connection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North%E2%80%93South_connection), linking the main railway stations in the city, was completed in 1952, while the first *[premetro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_premetro)* (underground tram) service was launched in 1969,[^cite_note-1960s-98] and the first [Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Metro) line was opened in 1976.[^cite_note-1970s-99] Starting from the early 1960s, Brussels became the *de facto* capital of what would become the [European Union](9317.md) (EU), and many modern offices were built. Development was allowed to proceed with little regard to the aesthetics of newer buildings, and numerous architectural landmarks were demolished to make way for newer buildings that often clashed with their surroundings, giving name to the process of [Brusselisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brusselisation).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEState200451–52-100][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStubbsMakaš2011121-101]

### Contemporary

The Brussels-Capital Region was formed on 18 June 1989, after a constitutional reform in 1988.[^cite_note-102] It is one of the three [federal regions of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_regions_of_Belgium), along with [Flanders](10878.md) and [Wallonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallonia), and has bilingual status.[^cite_note-Belgian_Constitution-15][^cite_note-CIRB-creation-16] The [yellow iris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_iris) is the region's emblem (referring to the presence of these flowers on the city's original site) and a stylised version is featured on its official flag.[^cite_note-103]

In the 21st century, Brussels has become an important venue for international events. In 2000, it was named [European Capital of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Capital_of_Culture) alongside eight other European cities.[^cite_note-104] In 2013, the city was the site of the [Brussels Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Agreement_\(2013\)).[^cite_note-105] In 2014, it hosted the [40th G7 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/40th_G7_summit),[^cite_note-\:0-106] and in 2017, 2018 and 2021 respectively the [28th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Brussels_summit), [29th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Brussels_summit) and [31st NATO Summits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Brussels_summit).[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108][^cite_note-\:9-109]

[On 22 March 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Brussels_bombings), three coordinated [nail bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nail_bomb) were detonated by [ISIL](9087364.md) in Brussels—two at [Brussels Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Airport) in [Zaventem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaventem) and one at [Maalbeek/Maelbeek metro station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maalbeek/Maelbeek_metro_station)—resulting in 32 victims and three [suicide bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_bombers) killed, and 330 people were injured. It was the deadliest act of [terrorism](30636.md) in Belgium.[^cite_note-independent-110][^cite_note-BBC35869985-111][^cite_note-BBC35880119-112][^cite_note-Guardian220317-113]

## Geography

### Location and topography

Further information: [Geography of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Belgium)



A 2020 satellite image of the Greater Brussels area

Brussels lies in the north-central part of Belgium, about 110 km (68 mi) from the Belgian coast and about 180 km (110 mi) from the country's southern tip. It is located in the heartland of the Brabantian Plateau, about 45 km (28 mi) south of [Antwerp](32149462.md) ([Flanders](10878.md)), and 50 km (31 mi) north of [Charleroi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charleroi) ([Wallonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallonia)). Its average [elevation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elevation) is 57 m (187 ft) above [sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_level), varying from a low point in the valley of the almost completely covered [Senne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senne_\(river\)), which cuts the Brussels-Capital Region from east to west, up to high points in the [Sonian Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonian_Forest), on its southeastern side. In addition to the Senne, tributary streams such as the [Maelbeek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maalbeek) and the [Woluwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woluwe), to the east of the region, account for significant elevation differences. [Brussels' central boulevards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Boulevards_of_Brussels) are 15 m (49 ft) above sea level.[^cite_note-114] Contrary to popular belief, the highest point (at 127.5 m (418 ft)) is not near the Place de l'Altitude Cent/Hoogte Honderdplein in [Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest,_Belgium), but at the Drève des Deux Montages/Tweebergendreef in the Sonian Forest.[^cite_note-115]

### Climate

Brussels experiences an [oceanic climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_climate) ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): *Cfb*) with warm summers and cool winters.[^cite_note-Brussels,+Brussels+Capital,+Belgium\&units=|title=Brussels,_Belgium_Climate_Classification_Köppen_(Weatherbase)-116] Proximity to coastal areas influences the area's climate by sending marine air masses from the [Atlantic Ocean](698.md). Nearby wetlands also ensure a maritime temperate climate. On average (based on measurements in the period 1981–2010), there are approximately 135 days of rain per year in the Brussels-Capital Region. Snowfall is infrequent, averaging 17 days per year. The city also often experiences violent thunderstorms in the summer months.

## As a capital

Despite its name, the Brussels-Capital Region is not the capital of [Belgium](3343.md). Article 194 of the [Belgian Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Constitution) establishes that the capital of Belgium is the [City of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Brussels), the municipality in the region that is the city's core.[^cite_note-article_194-17]

The City of Brussels is home to many national institutions. The [Royal Palace of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Palace_of_Brussels), where the [King of the Belgians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_the_Belgians) exercises his prerogatives as head of state,[^cite_note-121] is situated alongside [Brussels Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Park) (not to be confused with the [Royal Palace of Laeken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Palace_of_Laeken), the official home of the [Belgian royal family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Belgium)).[^cite_note-122] The [Palace of the Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_the_Nation) is located on the opposite side of this park, and is the seat of the [Belgian Federal Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Federal_Parliament).[^cite_note-123] The office of the [Prime Minister of Belgium](204622.md), colloquially called *Law Street 16* (French: *16, rue de la Loi*, Dutch: *Wetstraat 16*), is located adjacent to this building. It is also where the [Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_\(Belgium\)) holds its meetings. The [Court of Cassation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Cassation_\(Belgium\)), Belgium's main court, has its seat in the [Palace of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Justice,_Brussels). Other important institutions in the City of Brussels are the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Belgium), the [Council of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_State_\(Belgium\)), the [Court of Audit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Audit_of_Belgium), the [Royal Belgian Mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Belgian_Mint) and the [National Bank of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Belgium).

The City of Brussels is also the capital of both the [French Community of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Community_of_Belgium)[^cite_note-décret-18] and the [Flemish Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Community).[^cite_note-decreet-20] The [Flemish Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Parliament) and [Flemish Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Government) have their seats in Brussels,[^cite_note-124] and so do the [Parliament of the French Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_the_French_Community) and the [Government of the French Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_French_Community).



The [Royal Palace of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Palace_of_Brussels), the official palace of the [King and Queen of the Belgians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Belgium)

The 19 [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Belgium) (French: *communes*, Dutch: *gemeenten*) of the Brussels-Capital [Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communities,_regions_and_language_areas_of_Belgium#Regions) are political subdivisions with individual responsibilities for the handling of local level duties, such as law enforcement and the upkeep of schools and roads within its borders.[^cite_note-list-125][^cite_note-OECD-126] Municipal administration is also conducted by a mayor, a council, and an executive.[^cite_note-OECD-126]

In 1831, Belgium was divided into 2,739 municipalities, including the 19 currently located in the Brussels-Capital Region.[^cite_note-roots-127] Unlike most of the municipalities in Belgium, the ones now located in the Brussels-Capital Region were not merged with others during mergers occurring in 1964, 1970, and 1975.[^cite_note-roots-127] However, a few neighbouring municipalities have been merged into the [City of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Brussels), including [Laeken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laeken), [Haren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haren,_Belgium) and [Neder-Over-Heembeek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neder-Over-Heembeek) in 1921.[^cite_note-Brussels-128] These comprise the northern bulge in the municipality. To the south-east is a strip of land along the [Avenue Louise/Louizalaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avenue_Louise/Louizalaan) that, in addition to the [Bois de la Cambre/Ter Kamerenbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bois_de_la_Cambre/Ter_Kamerenbos), was annexed from [Ixelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ixelles) in 1864. Part of the [Université libre de Bruxelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_libre_de_Bruxelles) (ULB)'s [Solbosch/Solbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neighbourhoods_in_Brussels#Solbosch/Solbos) campus is also part of the City of Brussels, partially accounting for the bulge in the south-eastern end.

The largest municipality in area and population is the City of Brussels, covering 33.09 km<sup>2</sup> (12.8 sq mi) and with 198,314 inhabitants;[^cite_note-129] the least populous is [Koekelberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koekelberg) with 22,648 inhabitants.[^cite_note-130] The smallest in area is [Saint-Josse-ten-Noode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Josse-ten-Noode), which is only 1.16 km<sup>2</sup> (0.4 sq mi), but still has the highest population density in the region, with 23,486/km<sup>2</sup> (60,830/sq mi).[^cite_note-131] [Watermael-Boitsfort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watermael-Boitsfort) has the lowest population density in the region, with 1,953/km<sup>2</sup> (5,060/sq mi).[^cite_note-132]

There is much controversy on the division of 19 municipalities for a highly urbanised region, which is considered as (half of) one city by most people. Some politicians mock the "19 baronies" and want to merge the municipalities under one city council and one mayor.[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134] That would lower the number of politicians needed to govern Brussels, and centralise the power over the city to make decisions easier, thus reduce the overall running costs. The current municipalities could be transformed into districts with limited responsibilities, similar to the current structure of [Antwerp](32149462.md) or to structures of other capitals like the [boroughs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_boroughs) in [London](17867.md) or *[arrondissements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrondissements_of_Paris)* in [Paris](22989.md), to keep politics close enough to the citizen.[^cite_note-135]

In the 2010s, [Molenbeek-Saint-Jean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molenbeek-Saint-Jean) gained international attention as [the base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_ISIL_terror_cell) for [Islamist terrorists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_terrorists) who carried out attacks in both [Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks) and [Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Brussels_bombings).[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137][^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139][^cite_note-140]

## Brussels-Capital Region



Regions of Belgium:

&#x20;  [Flemish Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Region)

&#x20; Brussels-Capital Region

&#x20;  [Walloon Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walloon_Region)

### Political status and administration

See also: [Partition of Belgium § Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_Belgium#Brussels); and [Communities, regions and language areas of Belgium § Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communities,_regions_and_language_areas_of_Belgium#Brussels)

The Brussels-Capital Region is one of the three federated regions of Belgium, alongside the [Walloon Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walloon_Region) and the [Flemish Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Region). Geographically and linguistically, it is a bilingual [enclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enclave) in the monolingual Flemish Region. Regions are one component of Belgium's institutions; the three communities being the other component. Brussels' inhabitants deal with either the [French Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Community_of_Belgium) or the [Flemish Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Community) for matters such as culture and education, as well as a [Common Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Community_Commission) for competencies that do not belong exclusively to either Community, such as healthcare and social [welfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_spending).

Upon the reorganisation and split of the [Province of Brabant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Brabant) in 1995, the Brussels Region ceased to belong to any of the [provinces of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Belgium), nor was it subdivided into provinces itself. However, the [Arrondissement of Brussels-Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrondissement_of_Brussels-Capital), whose borders coincide with the Brussels Region, continued to exist, and the areas of provincial jurisdiction were assumed by the newly created [Governor of the Arrondissement of Brussels-Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_the_Administrative_Arrondissement_of_Brussels-Capital) and the Brussels regional institutions and community commissions. In 2014, the office of the Governor was formally abolished and replaced in September 2015 by the office of the "High official of the Arrondissement of Brussels-Capital", who retained responsibility for [crisis management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crisis_management) and [emergency planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_planning), while responsibility for [crime prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_prevention) was transferred to the [Minister-President of the Brussels-Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister-President_of_the_Brussels-Capital_Region).[^cite_note-High-141]

### Institutions

Main articles: [Government of the Brussels-Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_Brussels-Capital_Region) and [Parliament of the Brussels-Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_the_Brussels-Capital_Region)



The [Brussels Parliament building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Parliament_building), housing the [Brussels Regional Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Regional_Parliament)

The Brussels-Capital Region is governed by a [parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_the_Brussels-Capital_Region) of 89 members (72 French-speaking, 17 Dutch-speaking—parties are organised on a linguistic basis) and an eight-member regional cabinet consisting of a [minister-president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister-President_of_the_Brussels-Capital_Region), four ministers and three [state secretaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_state). The current [government under Boris Dilliès](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilli%C3%A8s_government) comprises seven parties across the social democrat, green, [liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism_in_Belgium) and conservative party spectrum. The coalition negotiations took almost two years, concluding 613 days after the elections and breaking the Belgian record for the longest time to form a government.[^cite_note-\:10-48]

By law, the cabinet must comprise two French-speaking and two Dutch-speaking ministers, one Dutch-speaking secretary of state and two French-speaking secretaries of state. The minister-president does not count against the language quota, but in practice every minister-president has been a bilingual francophone. The regional parliament can enact [ordinances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordinance_\(Belgium\)) (French: *ordonnances*, Dutch: *ordonnanties*), which have equal status as a national legislative act.

Nineteen of the 72 French-speaking members of the Brussels Parliament are also members of the [Parliament of the French Community of Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_the_French_Community_of_Belgium), and, until 2004, this was also the case for six Dutch-speaking members, who were at the same time members of the [Flemish Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Parliament). Now, people voting for a Flemish party have to vote separately for six directly elected members of the Flemish Parliament.

### Agglomeration

Before the creation of the Brussels-Capital Region, regional competences in the 19 municipalities were performed by the Brussels Agglomeration. The Brussels Agglomeration was an administrative division established in 1971. This decentralised administrative public body also assumed jurisdiction over areas that, elsewhere in Belgium, were exercised by municipalities or provinces.[^cite_note-142]

The Brussels Agglomeration had a separate legislative council, but the by-laws it enacted did not have legislative status. The only election of the council took place on 21 November 1971. The working of the council was subject to many difficulties caused by the linguistic and socio-economic tensions between the two communities.

After the creation of the Brussels-Capital Region, the Brussels Agglomeration was never formally abolished, although it no longer has a purpose.

## French and Flemish communities

Main articles: [French Community Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Community_Commission) and [Flemish Community Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Community_Commission)



Communities of Belgium:

&#x20;  [Flemish Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Community) / Dutch language area

&#x20; Flemish & French Community / bilingual language area

&#x20;  [French Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Community_of_Belgium) / French language area

&#x20;  [German-speaking Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German-speaking_Community) / German language area

The [French Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Community_of_Belgium) and the [Flemish Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Community) exercise their powers in Brussels through two community-specific public authorities: the [French Community Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Community_Commission) (French: *Commission communautaire française* or COCOF) and the [Flemish Community Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Community_Commission) (Dutch: *Vlaamse Gemeenschapscommissie* or VGC). These two bodies each have an assembly composed of the members from each linguistic group in the [Parliament of the Brussels-Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_the_Brussels-Capital_Region). They also have a board composed of the ministers and secretaries of state from each linguistic group in the Government of the Brussels-Capital Region.

The French Community Commission also has another capacity: some of the French Community's legislative powers have been devolved to the Walloon Region (for the French language area of Belgium) and to the French Community Commission (for the bilingual language area).[^cite_note-143] The Flemish Community, however, did the opposite; it merged the Flemish Region into the Flemish Community.[^cite_note-144] This is related the two linguistic groups having different conceptions, with one focusing more on the Communities and the other more on the Regions, causing an asymmetrical [federalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism). Because of this devolution, the French Community Commission can enact [decrees](322949.md), which are legislative acts.

### Common Community Commission

A bi-communitarian public authority, the [Common Community Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Community_Commission) (French: *Commission communautaire commune*, COCOM, Dutch: *Gemeenschappelijke Gemeenschapscommissie*, GGC) also exists. Its assembly is composed of the members of the regional parliament, and its board are the region's ministers—not the secretaries of state—with the minister-president not having the right to vote. This commission has two capacities: it is a decentralised administrative public body, responsible for implementing cultural policies of common interest. It can give subsidies and enact [by-laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/By-law). Additionally, it can also enact ordinances, which have equal status as a national legislative act, in the field of community welfare: in the Brussels-Capital Region, both the French Community and the Flemish Community can exercise powers in the field of welfare, but only in regard to institutions that are unilingual (for example, a private French-speaking retirement home or the Dutch-speaking hospital of the [Vrije Universiteit Brussel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vrije_Universiteit_Brussel)). The Common Community Commission is responsible for policies aiming directly at private persons or at bilingual institutions (for example, the centres for social welfare of the 19 municipalities). Its ordinances have to be enacted with a majority in both linguistic groups. Failing such a majority, a new vote can be held, where a majority of at least one third in each linguistic group is sufficient.

## Brussels and the European Union

Main article: [Brussels and the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_and_the_European_Union)



Aerial view of Brussels' [European Quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_and_the_European_Union#European_Quarter), hosting most of the [EU institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EU_institutions)

Brussels serves as *[de facto](43594.md)* capital of the [European Union](9317.md) (EU), hosting the major political [institutions of the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutions_of_the_European_Union).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemey2007-1] The EU has not declared a capital formally, though the [Treaty of Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Amsterdam) formally gives Brussels the seat of the [European Commission](9974.md) (the executive branch of government) and the [Council of the European Union](5866.md) (a legislative institution made up from executives of member states).[^cite_note-ENA_COMM_SEAT-145][^cite_note-EC_EiB-146] It locates the formal seat of [European Parliament](9581.md) in [Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strasbourg), where votes take place, with the council, on the proposals made by the Commission. However, meetings of political groups and committee groups are formally given to Brussels, along with a set number of plenary sessions. Three quarters of Parliament sessions now take place at its [Brussels hemicycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espace_L%C3%A9opold).[^cite_note-147] Between 2002 and 2004, the [European Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council) also fixed its seat in the city.[^cite_note-Dragoman-148] In 2014, the Union hosted a [G7 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/40th_G7_summit) in the city.[^cite_note-\:0-106]



The [Place du Luxembourg/Luxemburgplein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_du_Luxembourg) with the [European Parliament](9581.md) in the background

Brussels, along with [Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxembourg_City) and Strasbourg, began to host European institutions in 1957, soon becoming the centre of activities, as the Commission and Council based their activities in what has become the [European Quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_and_the_European_Union#European_Quarter), in the east of the city.[^cite_note-ENA_COMM_SEAT-145] Early building in Brussels was sporadic and uncontrolled, with little planning. The current major buildings are the [Berlaymont building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlaymont_building) of the Commission, symbolic of the quarter as a whole, the [Europa building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europa_building) of the Council and the [Espace Léopold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espace_L%C3%A9opold) of the Parliament.[^cite_note-EC_EiB-146] In recent years, the presence has increased considerably, with the Commission alone occupying 865,000 m<sup>2</sup> (9,310,000 sq ft) within the European Quarter (a quarter of the total office space in Brussels).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemey2007-1] The concentration and density has caused concern that the presence of the institutions has created a *[ghetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghetto) effect* in that part of the city.[^cite_note-EUO_buildings-149] However, the European presence has contributed significantly to the importance of Brussels as an international centre.[^cite_note-E!Sarp-150]

[Brussels and the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_and_the_European_Union)



The [Berlaymont building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlaymont_building) (**[European Commission](9974.md)**)



The [Europa building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europa_building) (**[European Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council)**)



The [Espace Léopold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espace_L%C3%A9opold) buildings (**[European Parliament](9581.md)**)

## International institutions

Brussels has, since [World War II](32927.md), become the administrative centre of many international organisations. The city is the political and administrative centre of the [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty_Organization) (NATO). [NATO's Brussels headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_headquarters) houses [29 embassies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_to_NATO) and brings together over 4,500 staff from allied nations, their militaries, and civil service personnel. Many other international organisations such as the [World Customs Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Customs_Organization) and [Eurocontrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocontrol), as well as international corporations, have their main institutions in the city. In addition, the main international [trade union](17626.md) confederations have their headquarters there: the [European Trade Union Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Trade_Union_Confederation) (ETUC), the [International Confederation of Free Trade Unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Confederation_of_Free_Trade_Unions) (ICFTU) and the [World Confederation of Labour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Confederation_of_Labour) (WCL).

Brussels is third in the number of international conferences it hosts,[^cite_note-ULB_Int-151] also becoming one of the largest convention centres in the world.[^cite_note-diplomatie-152] The presence of the EU and the other international bodies has, for example, led to there being more ambassadors and journalists in Brussels than in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md)[^cite_note-E!Sarp-150] The city hosts 120 international institutions, 181 [embassies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassies) (*intra muros*) and more than 2,500 [diplomats](28165911.md), making it the second centre of diplomatic relations in the world (after [New York City](645042.md)). [International schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_school) have also been established to serve this presence.[^cite_note-diplomatie-152] The "international community" in Brussels numbers at least 70,000 people.[^cite_note-153] In 2009, there were an estimated 286 [lobbying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobbying) consultancies known to work in Brussels.[^cite_note-154] Finally, Brussels has more than 1,400 [NGOs](46539.md).[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156]

### North Atlantic Treaty Organization

Main article: [NATO headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_headquarters)



Flags of [NATO member states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_NATO) wave at the entrance of [NATO's headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_headquarters) in [Haren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haren,_Belgium)

The [Treaty of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Brussels), which was signed on 17 March 1948 between Belgium, France, Luxembourg, the Netherlands and the United Kingdom, was a prelude to the establishment of the [intergovernmental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intergovernmental_organization) [military alliance](250766.md) that later became the [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty_Organization) (NATO).[^cite_note-157] As of 2024, the alliance consists of 32 independent member countries across [North America](21139.md) and Europe. Several countries also have diplomatic missions to NATO [through embassies in Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_to_NATO). Since 1949, a number of [NATO Summits](11190132.md) have been held in Brussels,[^cite_note-158] the most recent taking place in June 2021.[^cite_note-\:9-109] The organisation's political and administrative [headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_headquarters) are located on the Boulevard Léopold III/Leopold III-laan in [Haren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haren,_Belgium), on the north-eastern perimeter of the [City of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Brussels).[^cite_note-159] A new €750 million headquarters building begun in 2010 and was completed in 2017.[^cite_note-160]

### Eurocontrol

Main article: [Eurocontrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocontrol)

The European Organisation for the Safety of Air Navigation, commonly known as Eurocontrol, is an [international organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_organisation) that coordinates and plans [air traffic control](48563.md) across European [airspace](353557.md). The corporation was founded in 1960 and has 41 member states.[^cite_note-History_Book-161] Its headquarters are located in Haren, Brussels.

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Brussels)

### Population



[Population density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_density) of Europe. Brussels is located between the largest urban centres.

Brussels is located in one of the most [urbanised regions of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Banana), between [Paris](22989.md), [London](17867.md), the [Rhine-Ruhr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhine-Ruhr) (Germany), and the [Randstad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randstad) (Netherlands). The Brussels-Capital Region has a population of around 1.2 million and has witnessed, in recent years, a remarkable increase in its population. In general, the population of Brussels is younger than the national average, and the gap between rich and poor is wider.[^cite_note-162]

Brussels is the core of a built-up area that extends well beyond the region's limits. Sometimes referred to as the urban area of Brussels (French: *aire urbaine de Bruxelles*, Dutch: *stedelijk gebied van Brussel*) or Greater Brussels (French: *Grand-Bruxelles*, Dutch: *Groot-Brussel*), this area extends over a large part of the two Brabant provinces, including much of the surrounding [arrondissement of Halle-Vilvoorde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrondissement_of_Halle-Vilvoorde) and some small parts of the [arrondissement of Leuven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrondissement_of_Leuven) in [Flemish Brabant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Brabant), as well as the northern part of [Walloon Brabant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walloon_Brabant).

The metropolitan area of Brussels is divided into three levels. Firstly, the central agglomeration (French: *agglomération opérationnelle*, Dutch: *geoperationaliseerde agglomeratie*) within the regional borders, with an official population of 1,255,795 inhabitants and an additional 125,000 non-registered people.[^cite_note-\:12-163][^cite_note-164] Adding the closest suburbs (French: *banlieues*, Dutch: *buitenwijken*) gives a total population of 1,831,496. Including the outer [commuter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commuter) area (French: *zone résidentielle des migrants alternants*, Dutch: *forensenwoonzone*), the population is 2,676,701.[^cite_note-BelMetr-33][^cite_note-\:5-34] Brussels is also part of a wider [conurbation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conurbation) extending towards the cities of [Ghent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghent), [Antwerp](32149462.md), and [Leuven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leuven), known as the [Flemish Diamond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Diamond), as well as the province of Walloon Brabant, in total home to over 5 million people (a little more than 40% of Belgium's total population).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_MeeterenBoussauwDerudderWitlox2016-35][^cite_note-Meijers-2007-165]

There have been numerous migrations towards Brussels since the end of the 18th century, when the city acted as a common destination for [political refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_refugees) from neighbouring or more distant countries, particularly France.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumont1996307–8,_312,_319-167] From 1871, many of the [Paris Communards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communards) fled to Brussels, where they received political asylum. Other notable international exiles living in Brussels at the time included [Victor Hugo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Hugo), [Karl Marx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Marx), [Pierre-Joseph Proudhon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Joseph_Proudhon), [Georges Boulanger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_Boulanger), [Paul Verlaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Verlaine), [Arthur Rimbaud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Rimbaud), and [Léon Daudet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%A9on_Daudet), to name a few.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumont1996307–8,_312,_319-167][^cite_note-168] Attracted by the industrial opportunities, many workers moved in, first from the other [Belgian provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_provinces) (mainly rural residents from [Flanders](10878.md))[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuron201680–82-169] and France, then from [Southern European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Europe), and more recently from [Eastern European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe) and African countries.

Since the second half of the 20th century, Brussels has been home to a large number of immigrants and [émigré](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89migr%C3%A9) communities, as well as labour migrants, former foreign students or [expatriates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expatriate), and many Belgian families in Brussels can claim at least one foreign grandparent. According to [Statbel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statbel) (the Belgian Statistical Office), in 2020, taking into account the parents' nationality of birth, 74.3% of the population of the Brussels-Capital Region was of foreign origin and 41.8% was of non-European origin (including 28.7% of African origin). Among those aged under 18, 88% were of foreign origin and 57% of non-European origin (including 42.4% of African origin).[^cite_note-micheletribalat.fr-170] By 2024, citizens of 186 different nationalities were living in Brussels.[^cite_note-171] In 2025, of the region's 1,255,795 inhabitants, 788,740 (62.8%) were Belgian citizens, while 467,055 (37.2%) held a nationality other than Belgian. Citizens of other [European Union member states](10890716.md) formed the largest group of foreign nationals (291,627). The three municipalities with the most foreigners were Ixelles (51.0%), Etterbeek (49.8%), and Saint-Gilles (49.4%).[^cite_note-\:12-163]

The large concentration of immigrants and their descendants includes many of [Moroccan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moroccans_in_Belgium) (mainly [Riffian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riffian_people) and other [Berbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berbers)) and [Turkish](11125639.md) ancestry, together with French-speaking black Africans from former [Belgian colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_overseas_colonies), such as the Democratic Republic of the Congo. Many immigrants were [naturalised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturalised) following the great 1991 reform of the naturalisation process. Among all major migrant groups from outside the EU, a majority of the permanent residents have acquired Belgian nationality.[^cite_note-172]

### Languages

See also: [Francization of Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francization_of_Brussels)



Languages spoken at home in the Brussels-Capital Region (2013)

[^cite_note-brio2013-173]

\


&#x20; French

&#x20; French and Dutch

&#x20; Dutch

&#x20; French and other language

&#x20; Neither French nor Dutch

Brussels was historically [Dutch-speaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch-speaking), using the [Brabantian dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brabantian_dialect),[^cite_note-tokyo-174][^cite_note-jaumain-175][^cite_note-roegiest-176] but since the 19th century[^cite_note-tokyo-174][^cite_note-marynissen-177] [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_French) has become the city's predominant language.[^cite_note-rudi3-178] The main cause of this transition was the rapid [assimilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_assimilation) of the local [Flemish population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_people),[^cite_note-kramer-179][^cite_note-tokyo-174][^cite_note-baetens-180][^cite_note-ernst-181][^cite_note-roegiest-176] amplified by immigration from France and [Wallonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallonia).[^cite_note-tokyo-174][^cite_note-vermeersch-182] The [rise of French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francization) in public life gradually began by the end of the 18th century,[^cite_note-poirier-183][^cite_note-rousseaux-184] quickly accelerating after [Belgian independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_independence).[^cite_note-degroof-185][^cite_note-gubin-186] Dutch—of which [standardisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_language) in Belgium was still very weak[^cite_note-balans-187][^cite_note-busekist-188][^cite_note-gubin-186]—could not compete with French, which was the exclusive language of the judiciary, the administration, the army, education, cultural life and the media, and thus necessary for [social mobility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_mobility).[^cite_note-bitsch-189][^cite_note-tétart-190][^cite_note-jaumain-175][^cite_note-kok-191][^cite_note-marynissen-177] The value and prestige of the French language were universally acknowledged[^cite_note-jaumain-175][^cite_note-bogaert-192][^cite_note-kramer-179][^cite_note-gubin-186][^cite_note-hasquin2-193][^cite_note-vrints-194] to such an extent that after 1880,[^cite_note-veron-195][^cite_note-velthoven-196][^cite_note-balans-187] and more particularly after the turn of the 20th century,[^cite_note-gubin-186] proficiency in French among Dutch-speakers in Brussels increased spectacularly.[^cite_note-blampain-197]



[Bilingual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multilingualism) French and Dutch street signs in Brussels

Although a majority of the population remained bilingual until the second half of the 20th century,[^cite_note-blampain-197][^cite_note-kramer-179] family transmission of the historic Brabantian dialect[^cite_note-witte-198] declined,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETreffers-Daller1994-199] leading to an increase of monolingual French-speakers from 1910 onwards.[^cite_note-bogaert-192][^cite_note-metsenaere-thuis-200] From the mid-20th century, the number of monolingual French-speakers surpassed the number of mostly bilingual Flemish inhabitants.[^cite_note-brio4-201] This process of assimilation weakened after the 1960s,[^cite_note-blampain-197][^cite_note-mares-202] as the [language border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_border) was fixed, the status of Dutch as an official language of Belgium was reinforced,[^cite_note-depré-203] and the economic centre of gravity shifted northward to [Flanders](10878.md).[^cite_note-balans-187][^cite_note-veron-195] However, with the continuing arrival of immigrants and the post-war emergence of Brussels as a [centre of international politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_and_the_European_Union), the relative position of Dutch continued to decline.[^cite_note-brusselsstudies-204][^cite_note-marynissen-177][^cite_note-janssens-205][^cite_note-detant-206][^cite_note-blampain-197][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETreffers-Daller1994-199] Furthermore, as Brussels' urban area expanded,[^cite_note-meynen-207] a further number of Dutch-speaking municipalities in the [Brussels periphery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Periphery) also became predominantly French-speaking.[^cite_note-depré-203][^cite_note-klinkenberg-208] This phenomenon of expanding Francisation—dubbed "oil slick" by its opponents[^cite_note-kramer-179][^cite_note-kesteloot-209][^cite_note-blampain-197]—is, together with the future of Brussels,[^cite_note-frognier-210] one of the most controversial topics in [Belgian politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_politics).[^cite_note-veron-195][^cite_note-tétart-190]



The [municipalities with language facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_with_language_facilities) (in red) near Brussels

Since its creation in 1989, the Brussels-Capital Region has been legally bilingual, with both French and Dutch having official status,[^cite_note-constitution-211] as is the administration of the 19 municipalities.[^cite_note-brusselsstudies-204] The creation of this bilingual, full-fledged region, with its own competencies and jurisdiction, had long been hampered by different visions of Belgian federalism. Nevertheless, some communitarian issues remain.[^cite_note-jacobs-212][^cite_note-debruycker-213] Flemish political parties demanded, for decades, that the Flemish part of [Brussels-Halle-Vilvoorde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels-Halle-Vilvoorde) (BHV) *arrondissement* be separated from the Brussels Region (which made Halle-Vilvoorde a monolingual Flemish electoral and judicial district). BHV was divided mid-2012. The French-speaking population regards the language border as artificial[^cite_note-214] and demands the extension of the bilingual region to at least all six [municipalities with language facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_with_language_facilities) in the surroundings of Brussels.[^cite_note-215] Flemish politicians have strongly rejected these proposals.[^cite_note-question-216][^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218]
